# Garderobe requirement-to-code/test checklist

This checklist maps every requirement of the Garderobe rebuild to the code that implements it and the tests that prove it. Its sources are the revision 4 specification `requirements/garderobe-replacement-design.md` (SHA-256 `7f47ffd7aa5a7bcd92738c4eaa48d2ec0af79486440f490790007a8678558238`), the owner profile `requirements/chris-wardrobe-profile.md` (SHA-256 `e15639d891f9a5264c7eff13d05a478bcb188745aea11323b2131db37f5cb198`), the September 15 owner amendments `requirements/support/wardrobe-support/evals/sources/owner-amendments.md` (they supersede conflicting specification text), the evaluation instructions `requirements/support/wardrobe-support/evals/README.md` and `judge.md`, the usage research `requirements/support/wardrobe-support/handoff/support/garderobe-usage-research.md` (requirements R1 to R59), and the owner's kickoff instructions `requirements/support/wardrobe-start-prompt.md`. Every row starts with `Code` and `Tests` set to `—` and `Status` set to `open`; the workstream named in the row fills them in under the rules below. `Source` gives the section and line number(s) in the source file (`§` is a specification or profile section, `L` a line number).

## How to use

- Each workstream fills in `Code` (repository-relative paths, optionally with symbols) and `Tests` (test file and test name) for the rows where it is the primary or a secondary workstream, and changes `Status` to `implemented` (code and passing local tests cited), `partial` (say what is missing in the Requirement cell's trailing parenthesis), `blocked` (needs a credential, entitlement, device or macOS toolchain — name it), or leaves it `open`.
- Never mark a row `implemented` on the strength of a mock of the thing the row is about; cloud, device and Xcode evidence is recorded only when actually run.
- Rows are never deleted; a superseded requirement keeps its row with status `superseded` and a pointer to the superseding row.
- Edit only your own rows' `Code`, `Tests` and `Status` cells.

## Workstreams

The `Workstream` cell holds exactly one primary key, optionally followed by secondary keys after a `+` (for example `foundation+daily-service`).

| Key | Covers |
| --- | --- |
| `foundation` | Monorepo, shared versioned contracts, base D1 schema, the domain ledger and single command service (receipts, idempotency, preconditions and expected versions, undo as compensation, outbox and effect records), garments, aliases, facts, stock quantities and movements, restrictions and locations, wear observations and per-garment-per-wearing-date counting, authoritative owner observations and event-order accounting repair, laundry batches, weekly reset, exceptions and hand-wash, the probabilistic availability estimator, style documents, amendments, rules, standing directions and temporary briefs, measurement and fit fact storage, per-owner isolation in D1, import of the profile and inventory CSV with reconciliation report, synthetic second owner fixture, document hashes, the local D1 test harness. |
| `daily-service` | Mandatory context assembly for recommendations, weather skill and adapters, calendar reading and influence, board composition, validation, repair and publication, boards, options, selections and saved combinations, variety and repeat rules at composition, evening-to-morning schedule and due-job sweep, Calendar projection of the managed event, pause and resume, trips and packing, the private web board. |
| `assistant` | Think and Durable Object conversation runtime, turn queueing, compaction, recall and AI Search retrieval, Agent Memory, inference, model registry, AI Gateway, budgets and reservations, research and shopping investigations, purchases from email (Gmail), web search, Tavily, Exa and Browser Run, history and provenance research, lifecycle projects (consignment, tailoring, disposal), return and exchange deadlines, comfort feedback interpretation, outbound MCP connections, Drive and Sheets, deletion and forgetting of conversation sources. |
| `api-mcp-identity` | The HTTP API endpoints (`/v1/...`), SSE run events, the MCP server and its tools, the MCP 2026-07-28 contract, MCP OAuth authorization for Claude and ChatGPT, Cloudflare Access login and identity mapping, `auth_identities`, connection endpoints, account recovery credential flow, portable export, backups and restore, route trust boundaries. |
| `visual-wardrobe` | Media uploads, private R2 storage, image discovery and backfill, catalogue normalization, cutouts, fidelity checks, outfit composition manifests and rendering, Studio backend support, image privacy and retention. |
| `ios` | The native SwiftUI app: Today, Wardrobe, Studio, Conversation, capture, Laundry sheet, settings, accessibility, offline cache and queued commands, restoration, native OAuth client, share extension, first-use flow, design-skill requirements. |
| `journey-tests` | Functional and UX journey test suite, integration of the 64-case evaluation corpus, candidate and judge isolation, behavioural state assertions, acceptance-table cases that are end-to-end journeys. |
| `adversarial-tests` | Adversarial suite: hallucinated items, forged owner fields, prompt injection from untrusted content, concurrency races, replay and duplicate attacks, SSRF, isolation probes, negative stock attempts. |
| `deployment` | Cloudflare resource provisioning, wrangler configuration, environments, gateways and spend limits, secrets, free-plan measurements, cost and budget operations, observability, real-platform acceptance probes, Apple distribution, cutover. |
| `simulation` | The full seeded MCP simulation with random outfit selections and simulated availability, weather, calendar and circumstance. |

| S10-061 | §10 L647 | The assistant explores a hypothesis without endorsing its premise, states uncertainty where sources do not establish a connection, and never invents a historical lineage. | assistant+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |

| S10-063 | §10 L653 | Lifecycle work is a durable project holding items, photographs, listing copy, known prices, intended destination and next action; a consignment proceeds from inventory selection through photo matching, copy, prepared web form, pickup and recorded proceeds. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S10-064 | §10 L653 | The assistant minimizes physical and administrative steps, including the owner's preference for collection over packaging and couriers. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S10-065 | §10 L655 | For-sale stock is excluded from ordinary recommendations but remains owned until a completed pickup records its departure. | S10-067 | §10 L657 | Browser actions in lifecycle work use a bounded session attached to the project; read-only research and form preparation run autonomously, while sending a message, submitting a listing, purchasing or committing to a service requires authorization for that concrete action. | assistant+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S10-068 | §10 L657 | Existing authorization is retained so the same decision is not asked repeatedly, and a challenge needing human interaction produces a phone-accessible handoff at the prepared step. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S10-069 | §10 L659 | Shopping, research and lifecycle capabilities are part of functional parity and are not dropped in favour of the daily board. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S10-070 | §10 L663 | A trip records departure and return dates, destinations, local timezones, important occasions, luggage limits and available laundry, and can be started from an ordinary sentence. | daily-service | packages/daily/src/trips.ts; packages/contracts/src/ext/daily.ts | packages/daily/test/trips.test.ts | implemented |

| S10-082 | §10 L671 | Preparing a return follows existing authorization, while a paid booking or external submission follows the concrete action policy. | assistant | — | — | open |

## Specification section 11 — The visual wardrobe

| ID | Source | Requirement | Workstream | Code | Tests | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| S11-001 | §11 L683 | The visual catalogue is a separate pipeline from outfit composition, building a reusable traceable asset per garment; an asset record distinguishes exact product photograph, owner photograph, edited rendition and generic illustration. | visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S11-002 | §11 L687 | Discovery step 1: inspect the purchase source and stored product link for the exact product, fabric code, colourway and variant. | visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S11-003 | §11 L688 | Discovery step 2: inspect the manufacturer's product and archive pages, then retailers carrying that exact version. | visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S11-004 | §11 L689 | Discovery step 3: search by identifiers and distinguishing attributes using search and image-search providers, examining the source page behind an image result. | visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S11-005 | §11 L690 | Discovery step 4: compare candidates with the recorded garment and owner photographs and reject wrong colourways, different generations, materially different cuts and uncertain lookalikes. | visual-wardrobe+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S11-006 | §11 L691 | Discovery step 5: choose the best verified image, create catalogue renditions, and retain source URL, retrieval date, content hash and match evidence. | visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S11-007 | §11 L692 | Discovery step 6: if the bounded investigation cannot establish a match, add the item to Photos needed with one sentence describing the useful photograph. | visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S11-008 | §11 L694 | The initial per-garment discovery allowance is three search strategies, up to 12 candidate pages and at most two browser sessions, spread across durable jobs and the configured daily budget, with exact-identifier candidates ranked first. | visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S11-009 | §11 L694 | A retry uses a new source or a supplied photo and never repeats the same unsuccessful search indefinitely; the batch prioritizes active garments with high expected use. | visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S11-010 | §11 L694 | The backfill shows an estimated completion range, reserves interactive browser capacity first, and offers a bounded paid Browser Run budget for a faster backfill. | visual-wardrobe+deployment | — | — | open |
| S11-011 | §11 L696 | Automatic adoption requires strong product-identity evidence and passing image-quality checks; an uncalibrated model confidence number never promotes an uncertain candidate. | visual-wardrobe+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S11-012 | §11 L696 | The owner sees only exceptions needing a decision, grouped into one short review; image-source and permitted-use metadata are retained and the catalogue stays private. | visual-wardrobe+ios | — | — | open |
| S11-013 | §11 L700 | The original image is immutable; a background-removed cutout and, where supported, a normalized front view are produced and stored with outfit composites in R2. | visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S11-014 | §11 L700 | Display thumbnails are generated on demand through Cloudflare Images at a fixed set of dimensions and cached at delivery, not precomputed in R2 unless a measured need justifies it. | visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S11-015 | §11 L700 | Catalogue renditions use a consistent neutral canvas, generous margins, realistic proportions and restrained shadows, with transparent cutouts for outfit layouts. | visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S11-016 | §11 L702 | Background removal preserves source pixels where practical; rotation, cropping and lighting correction precede generative editing, which receives the actual image and explicit constraints to preserve colour, pattern scale, pockets, buttons, seams and silhouette. | visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S11-017 | §11 L704 | Generatively edited output is a derived representation with a fidelity check against the original covering identity, dominant colours, important details, clipping, halos and missing components; a materially changed result is rejected. | visual-wardrobe+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S11-018 | §11 L704 | A view that reconstructs unseen parts or changes drape is marked as edited and never used as evidence for fabric or fit. | visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S11-019 | §11 L706 | A generic illustration is permitted only when no accurate asset exists, is labelled Illustration in item details and on the outfit, and can never silently become an exact garment asset. | visual-wardrobe+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S11-020 | §11 L708 | Image generation and editing are provider capabilities behind the model service, not tied to a particular image model or consumer subscription. | visual-wardrobe+assistant | — | — | open |
| S11-021 | §11 L712 | An outfit image is a deterministic arrangement of approved assets (top and trousers in the main column, shoes beneath, outerwear beside or partially layered, accessories in consistent positions). | visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S11-022 | §11 L712 | Layout templates handle long coats, short jackets, knitwear and one-piece garments; relative sizing is category-based unless trustworthy dimensions allow better proportions. | visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S11-023 | §11 L714 | The composition manifest stores item IDs, rendition versions, positions, scale, layering order, background and template version, and its content hash identifies the cached output. | visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S11-024 | §11 L714 | A single-slot swap changes one asset reference and invalidates only the affected composite, with no image model redrawing the outfit. | visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S11-025 | §11 L716 | SwiftUI renders the manifest interactively; the backend can render an SVG scene from private controlled asset references and export a raster preview through the image/browser rendering adapter. | visual-wardrobe+ios | — | — | open |
| S11-026 | §11 L716 | Generated SVG accepts no arbitrary markup from models or external pages. | visual-wardrobe+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S11-027 | §11 L716 | Completed previews are stored in R2; rendering is background work that cannot delay the morning board. | visual-wardrobe+daily-service | — | — | open |
| S11-028 | §11 L718 | The composition is presented as a faithful view of which pieces are combined, not a body-fit simulation; on-body visualization is a later separate feature labelled as an approximation, and a selfie can sit beside the catalogue composition. | visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S11-029 | §11 L722 | Private R2 stores originals, cutouts, edited images, thumbnails, outfit previews and selfie attachments; D1 holds identities and metadata, never image bytes. | visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S11-030 | §11 L722 | Uploads use short-lived authorization, size limits, content validation and an explicit finalization step before an image enters a task; the backend validates even client-resized uploads. | visual-wardrobe+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S11-031 | §11 L724 | Images are served via authenticated requests or narrowly scoped short-lived URLs; a public calendar link never embeds a permanent bearer URL to a selfie. | visual-wardrobe+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S11-032 | §11 L724 | A provider receives only the relevant image for its job, never the whole bucket; location metadata is stripped from derivatives. | visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S11-033 | §11 L724 | Full selfies are retained only according to the owner's photo-history setting; faces and backgrounds are not needed for cutouts. | visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S11-034 | §11 L726 | Default retention keeps catalogue originals while their garments remain in the collection, keeps outfit-history thumbnails, and offers a configurable retention period for full-resolution selfies. | visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S11-035 | §11 L726 | Media deletion propagates through derived assets, model-file references where supported, caches and the backup retention policy. | visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S11-036 | §11 L726 | A face is never used to infer personality or change the style profile. | visual-wardrobe+assistant+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |

## Specification section 12 — Configurable inference

| ID | Source | Requirement | Workstream | Code | Tests | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| S12-001 | §12 L732 | Every application-owned inference call passes through Cloudflare AI Gateway: conversation, recommendations, receipt extraction, vision, compaction, memory enrichment, embeddings, image generation and image editing. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S12-002 | §12 L732 | Code Mode and Sandbox jobs call the same backend model service and never receive a provider key; models invoked through third-party tools need an explicit compatible routing contract or stay disabled. | assistant+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S12-003 | §12 L732 | AI Gateway is the accounting boundary for retries and fallbacks, with Garderobe run and task IDs attached as non-sensitive metadata. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S12-004 | §12 L734 | Cloudflare Unified Billing is used for eligible routes; a model profile becomes selectable only after an authenticated capability and billing probe succeeds, and unsupported combinations stay visible as unavailable with their precise reason. | assistant+deployment | — | — | open |
| S12-005 | §12 L734 | There is no silent direct-provider or BYOK fallback that violates the owner's billing preference. | assistant+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S12-006 | §12 L736 | Provider keys are omitted from requests and default BYOK settings for these gateways, and Workers AI routes are configured to use Unified Billing where applicable; a credit balance is not treated as an absolute spending cap. | assistant+deployment | — | — | open |
| S12-007 | §12 L738 | Dedicated `garderobe-dev` and `garderobe-prod` gateways exist in the selected account with separate credentials, traffic metadata, logs and application budgets. | deployment | — | — | open |
| S12-008 | §12 L738 | Prompt and response body logging is disabled by default; usage and latency metrics are kept without email contents, selfies or the profile. | deployment+assistant | — | — | open |
| S12-009 | §12 L738 | Caching is disabled for personalized generative responses initially; any later cache includes owner, model, profile version, domain snapshot and relevant source versions in its isolation key. | assistant+deployment | — | — | open |
| S12-010 | §12 L740 | The Worker-side AI binding is used where supported and the allowed Gateway IDs are fixed in trusted configuration; the backend model service enforces the permitted route. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S12-011 | §12 L740 | Gateway Run tokens are never exposed to a model, Sandbox, MCP consumer or phone. | assistant+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S12-012 | §12 L742 | The intended gateway is always named; implicit creation of `default` is never relied on. | assistant+deployment | — | — | open |
| S12-013 | §12 L744 | The model registry records exact provider route, API model ID, supported operations, effective effort, context limits, rate limits, data permissions, price observation date, Gateway ID, Unified Billing probe result and fallback order. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S12-014 | §12 L744 | Text, tools, vision and image editing are probed independently per route. | assistant+deployment | — | — | open |
| S12-015 | §12 L746 | Spend is reserved in the D1 command ledger before each application-controlled model call (covering maximum output, retry allowance, compaction, regeneration after deletions and tool charges), settled against actual usage, with uncertain reservations retained until reconciliation. | assistant+foundation | — | — | open |
| S12-016 | §12 L746 | Bulk image work stays below a separate limit and tomorrow's board budget is reserved. | assistant+daily-service | — | — | open |
| S12-017 | §12 L746 | Automatic refill stays disabled for a newly dedicated billing arrangement unless the owner selects a bounded policy; the shared account's refill state is inspected before unattended traffic and account-wide policy is not altered by assumption. | deployment | — | — | open |
| S12-018 | §12 L749 | The `onCompaction` callback, proactive compaction, overflow recovery, deletion-driven regeneration and fallback attempts all use the application model service and obtain a reservation before inference; this is covered by the Think integration test. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S12-019 | §12 L749 | Exhausting the budget leaves a durable, resumable turn and never triggers an unbudgeted harness request. | assistant+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S12-020 | §12 L749 | For managed AI Search operations the application reserves a bounded job allowance, caps input size and optional rewrite/rerank work, and reconciles from Gateway usage; no route silently switches to account-invoiced Workers AI. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S12-021 | §12 L753 | Model selection is backend configuration visible in the app; a profile contains provider, exact API model ID, supported input types, effort parameters, output limits, timeouts, budget and ordered fallback profiles, changeable per task without changing the iOS app. | assistant+ios | — | — | open |
| S12-022 | §12 L753-L755 | Candidate providers (DeepSeek V4.1 Flash, Kimi, GLM, Fable 5.1, a verified OpenAI model) are pending probes; the first passing Unified Billing conversation model is selected and no profile is claimed enabled before its probe. | assistant+deployment | — | — | open |
| S12-023 | §12 L759 | Task profile (pending probe): conversation and style advice uses the DeepSeek V4.1 Flash candidate, falling back to a tested heavyweight profile if quality, tool behaviour or latency fails the task's limits. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S12-024 | §12 L760 | Task profile (pending probe): evening outfit composition uses the best passing profile from wardrobe evaluations, then a second passing provider, then revalidated approved combinations. | daily-service+assistant | packages/daily/src/compose.ts; packages/daily/src/ports.ts | packages/daily/test/board.test.ts; note - model port with validated proposals, approved combinations and the deterministic fallback implemented; profile selection from evaluations is the assistant workstream and pending its probe | partial |
| S12-025 | §12 L761 | Task profile (pending probe): receipt and page extraction uses a low-cost structured extraction profile, escalating to a more capable extraction or vision profile for ambiguous material. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S12-026 | §12 L762 | Task profile (pending probe): photo matching uses a profile verified for image input and wardrobe matching, then a stronger vision profile, then one human disambiguation. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S12-027 | §12 L763 | Task profile (pending probe): historical research uses a research-capable profile with application-owned retrieval, falling back to another passing profile over the same source set. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S12-028 | §12 L764 | Task profile (pending probe): catalogue editing uses a dedicated image-editing provider; on failure the original is retained or the asset marked unresolved. | visual-wardrobe+assistant | — | — | open |
| S12-029 | §12 L765 | Task profile (pending probe): compaction and recall enrichment use a tested summarization profile through AI Gateway, retrying or switching to an eligible model without dropping source history. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S12-030 | §12 L766 | Task profile (pending probe): AI Search semantic indexing uses an embedding model verified through Gateway and Unified Billing; lexical and entity search stay available during indexing outages. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S12-031 | §12 L767 | Routine native commands use no model and run in a deterministic command handler. | foundation | packages/domain/src/commands/service.ts; packages/domain/src/commands/registry.ts; migrations/0001_foundation.sql (commands, command_preconditions) | packages/domain/test/command-service.test.ts | implemented |
| S12-032 | §12 L769 | The resolved provider response (actual model behind an alias such as `deepseek-flash`) is recorded, and published speed figures are treated as hypotheses. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S12-033 | §12 L771 | Kimi and GLM are supported as configurable provider families with exact models and parameter contracts verified when a profile is enabled. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S12-034 | §12 L773 | Fable 5.1 (`claude-fable-5-1`) is an explicit heavyweight fallback candidate; "GPT Extra" stays pending an exact API ID rather than being invented, and GPT-6 Astra (`gpt-6-astra`) is offered as a separate option not equated with it. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S12-035 | §12 L777 | The provider adapter normalizes messages, tool calls, structured output, image inputs, usage, errors and cancellation while preserving provider-specific reasoning settings; a saved profile states exact effective request parameters and unsupported parameters are rejected at profile test time. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S12-036 | §12 L779 | The application performs mandatory reads itself rather than depending on forced tool use, and each adapter has contract tests against the actual model endpoint. | assistant+deployment | — | — | open |
| S12-037 | §12 L781 | Fallback occurs only on transport errors, timeouts, invalid structured output after bounded repair, or too few valid candidates, never because an answer is unwelcome but supported. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S12-038 | §12 L781 | Provider and model versions, effort, prompt version, schema version and input evidence are recorded for every run. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S12-039 | §12 L783 | On a provider change the conversation is reconstructed from ordinary messages, current domain context and committed tool receipts; private reasoning blocks are not forwarded and completed commands are not reissued. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S12-040 | §12 L783 | A paid image request with unknown outcome is reconciled with the provider job or flagged as an uncertain charge before any retry. | assistant+visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S12-041 | §12 L785 | A circuit breaker avoids repeatedly calling an unhealthy provider, and separate task budgets reserve capacity for the daily board, interactive conversation, research and image backfill. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S12-042 | §12 L785 | The owner can choose a model for experimentation, but promotion to the scheduled morning profile requires passing the same tests as the previous profile. | assistant+journey-tests | — | — | open |

| ID | Source | Requirement | Workstream | Code | Tests | Status |

| S13-001 | §13 L791 | The HTTP API returns versioned domain objects, never model-written interface markup. | api-mcp-identity | — | — | open |

| S13-004 | §13 L797 | `GET /v1/items/{id}` reads an item with its facts and media using stable identities and dated evidence. | api-mcp-identity | — | — | open |
| S13-005 | §13 L798 | `POST /v1/commands` executes a typed domain change with idempotency key and expected versions and returns a verified receipt. | api-mcp-identity+foundation | — | — | open |
| S13-006 | §13 L799 | `POST /v1/conversation/turns` appends to the owner's continuous conversation through Think with a stable client turn ID, text, attachment IDs and attached item or outfit references. | api-mcp-identity+assistant | — | — | open |
| S13-007 | §13 L800 | `GET /v1/conversation/messages` pages through the canonical transcript with a stable message cursor, surrounding-message retrieval and source-channel metadata. | api-mcp-identity+assistant | — | — | open |
| S13-008 | §13 L801 | `POST /v1/recall/search` searches dated conversations and personal judgments with date bounds, entity and text queries, evidence links and the index watermark. | api-mcp-identity+assistant | — | — | open |
| S13-009 | §13 L802 | `GET /v1/runs/{id}` retrieves durable progress or result and works after app closure or a lost stream. | api-mcp-identity | — | — | open |
| S13-010 | §13 L803 | `GET /v1/runs/{id}/events` observes a run with server-sent events whose ordered event IDs support reconnection. | api-mcp-identity | — | — | open |
| S13-011 | §13 L804 | `POST /v1/runs/{id}/cancel` cancels remaining work and returns committed effects and what was stopped. | api-mcp-identity | — | — | open |
| S13-012 | §13 L805 | `POST /v1/uploads` authorizes a media upload and returns bounded upload authorization and an upload ID. | api-mcp-identity+visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S13-013 | §13 L806 | `POST /v1/uploads/{id}/complete` validates and finalizes an upload; unfinalized files cannot become garment evidence. | api-mcp-identity+visual-wardrobe | — | — | open |
| S13-014 | §13 L807 | `GET /v1/connections` reads connection health and capabilities without showing secrets and supports phone recovery. | api-mcp-identity | — | — | open |
| S13-015 | §13 L808 | `POST /v1/connections` registers an owner-chosen MCP connection with endpoint validation, auth flow and selected capability policy. | api-mcp-identity+assistant | — | — | open |
| S13-016 | §13 L809 | `POST /v1/connections/{id}/disconnect` revokes local access, stops future calls, cancels queued calls, clears sessions and reports the remote revocation outcome. | api-mcp-identity+assistant | — | — | open |
| S13-017 | §13 L810 | `GET /v1/settings` reads profile and delivery configuration including effective model profile, budget and version. | api-mcp-identity | — | — | open |
| S13-018 | §13 L812 | A command receipt contains `commandId`, `outcome`, affected entity versions, a concise verified summary and external-effect state; a long operation returns a `runId` and `accepted` is never rendered as done. | api-mcp-identity+foundation | — | — | open |
| S13-019 | §13 L814 | The event stream uses semantic events `run_started`, `activity`, `text_delta`, `outfit_board`, `product_comparison`, `sources`, `command_receipt`, `needs_input` and `run_finished`; activity describes visible work, not raw reasoning. | api-mcp-identity | — | — | open |
| S13-020 | §13 L814 | Stream replay uses ordered event IDs and a durable final result; event retention is bounded and an expired cursor receives a snapshot rather than a silent gap. | api-mcp-identity | — | — | open |
| S13-021 | §13 L816 | Streamed prose cannot announce an uncommitted mutation as complete, and outfit cards appear only after validation; speculative text cannot produce an actionable garment card. | api-mcp-identity+assistant+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S13-022 | §13 L816 | The app tolerates unknown event types and uses the API schema version to stay compatible with backend additions. | ios | — | — | open |

| S13-059 | §13 L868 | Local stdio servers require an explicit remote bridge or bounded Sandbox host and never run on the owner's Mac as a hidden production dependency. | assistant | — | — | open |

(D1 commands and reads are owner-scoped and tested; R2, AI Search, memory, exports and grants are their workstreams) | foundation+visual-wardrobe+assistant | packages/domain/src/platform.ts; packages/domain/src/principal.ts; migrations/0001_foundation.sql | packages/domain/test/isolation-style-platform.test.ts | partial |
| S15-020 | §15 L991 | A background result rechecks ownership before entering a conversation. | assistant+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S15-021 | §15 L993 | An identity can be unlinked without deleting the wardrobe or its provider connections; account deletion is a separate operation. | api-mcp-identity | — | — | open |
| S15-022 | §15 L993 | Google login and Gmail/Calendar consent are separate grants: an expired app session does not cancel unattended preparation and disconnecting Gmail does not prevent app login, while disabling the account or revoking scheduled authority stops future external effects. | api-mcp-identity+daily-service | — | — | open |
| S15-023 | §15 L996 | Google Workspace access is a backend connection: authorization launches in the system browser from iOS, the authorization-code flow completes server-side, the offline refresh grant stays on the backend, and the request is bound to the signed-in owner with state. | api-mcp-identity+assistant | — | — | open |
| S15-024 | §15 L996 | The phone can disconnect or reconnect Gmail, Calendar, Drive and other MCP services from settings without copying a secret from a terminal; a token from one connection is never forwarded to an unrelated MCP server. | ios+assistant+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S15-025 | §15 L998 | Requested Google scopes are Gmail read, Calendar read, and write only for the dedicated outfit calendar and authorized reminders; the backend additionally enforces calendar and operation allowlists, and sending email or editing arbitrary calendars is not permitted. | assistant+daily-service+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S15-026 | §15 L1000 | A dedicated Google Cloud project with an External production consent configuration is used; the personal-use verification exception is validated for the requested scopes and recorded before launch, and verification is pursued if it does not apply. | deployment | — | — | open |
| S15-027 | §15 L1002 | Acceptance verifies the deployed consent state, a refresh after more than seven days without re-consent, and deliberate revocation and recovery. | deployment | — | — | open |
| S15-028 | §15 L1004 | Gateway runtime credentials and search API keys are stored in Secrets Store or Worker secrets; Google and other MCP grants are encrypted at rest with a separately managed key; credentials are never returned through chat, MCP resources, diagnostic bundles or logs. | deployment+api-mcp-identity+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S15-029 | §15 L1004 | The model receives only the minimum relevant email excerpts, calendar context and image attachments, and model profiles record which providers (including fallbacks) may receive each data class. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S15-030 | §15 L1006 | External emails, product pages, PDFs and image metadata are untrusted: their contents cannot change system prompts, reveal tokens, invoke unrelated tools or alter inventory. | assistant+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S15-031 | §15 L1006 | Fetching a user-supplied URL enforces destination restrictions, redirect checks, timeouts and response limits; public research and private mailbox access use separate adapters and permissions. | assistant+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S15-032 | §15 L1008 | Connection health is checked before the evening run and before morning delivery; recoverable token errors refresh automatically and revoked grants produce one clear reconnect action. | assistant+daily-service | — | — | open |
| S15-033 | §15 L1008 | Failures are isolated: a failed MCP authorization does not affect native sign-in, the scheduler or Google grants, and a failed Google grant does not prevent reading the wardrobe or logging a wear. | api-mcp-identity+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S15-034 | §15 L1010 | The recovery screen shows the last board, last confirmed Calendar projection, pending commands and connection issues with Reconnect, Retry or Open today's board, with diagnostics behind a separate disclosure and no builder-owned token needed. | ios+api-mcp-identity | — | — | open |
| S15-035 | §15 L1014 | Account setup provides a one-time recovery credential with a downloadable recovery kit and storage instruction; only its cryptographic verifier is stored, separate from login tokens. | api-mcp-identity | — | — | open |
| S15-036 | §15 L1014 | The recovery flow on the dedicated authentication route proves possession of the credential, binds an alternative verified identity to the existing internal user ID, invalidates the used credential, revokes prior native sessions and consumer assistant grants, and issues a replacement kit. | api-mcp-identity | — | — | open |
| S15-037 | §15 L1014 | An authenticated owner can link a second identity in advance; matching email, biographical answers or wardrobe knowledge never prove ownership. | api-mcp-identity+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S15-038 | §15 L1016 | Recovery uses established cryptographic libraries, rate limits, expiring recovery transactions and audit receipts; it cannot restore a revoked Google grant. | api-mcp-identity+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S15-039 | §15 L1016 | The complete loss-of-Google-access journey is proven on a test owner without deleting wardrobe data, bypassing ownership or relying on a valid Access session; if neither linked identity nor credential survives, the system reports it cannot recover and invents no support override. | api-mcp-identity+journey-tests | — | — | open |
| S15-040 | §15 L1020 | Export my wardrobe produces one downloadable private package with a versioned manifest, checksums, export time, store watermarks and documented JSON records. | api-mcp-identity | — | — | open |
| S15-041 | §15 L1020 | The export includes inventory and aliases, observations and quantity movements, daily wears and amendments, profile originals and amendments, standing rules, saved combinations and boards, orders, trips, lifecycle projects, research and source references, original conversation messages with dates, and media with transformation provenance. | api-mcp-identity | — | — | open |
| S15-042 | §15 L1020 | The export includes readable Markdown or HTML and CSV views and a README explaining relationships, units, date semantics, unresolved estimates and missing external attachments. | api-mcp-identity | — | — | open |
| S15-043 | §15 L1020 | Credentials, recovery verifiers, browser cookies and raw internal reasoning are excluded from the export. | api-mcp-identity+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S15-044 | §15 L1022 | The export is a resumable job over a coherent source snapshot or version boundary; incomplete components are marked explicitly and a partial download is never reported as complete. | api-mcp-identity | — | — | open |
| S15-045 | §15 L1022 | The export supports an optional encrypted package and short-lived authenticated download, and applies deletion tombstones and current source permissions. | api-mcp-identity | — | — | open |
| S15-046 | §15 L1022 | A portability test verifies checksums and imports the package into an empty isolated owner fixture without querying the old service, silently changing IDs or replaying external effects; it is separate from backup restoration. | api-mcp-identity+journey-tests | — | — | open |
| S15-047 | §15 L1024 | Backups preserve D1 exports, Think Session messages and compaction overlays, connector metadata, source assets and manifests with bounded retention, and a restore manifest records snapshot times and projection watermarks across stores. | api-mcp-identity+deployment | — | — | open |
| S15-048 | §15 L1024 | A restore drill recovers identities, quantities, wear revisions, style versions, the continuous transcript, media links, pending turns, deletion tombstones and effects, and rebuilds recall indexes before reporting complete search coverage. | api-mcp-identity+deployment | — | — | open |
| S15-049 | §15 L1024 | Backup restoration never re-sends already completed calendar or external operations; receipts and remote reconciliation prevent duplication. | api-mcp-identity+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |

(shared contracts and the single command service done; the other layers are the named secondary workstreams) | foundation+api-mcp-identity+assistant+deployment | packages/contracts/src/; packages/contracts/generated/ | packages/contracts/test/contracts.test.ts | partial |

| S17-052 | §17 L1135 | A restored backup replays tombstones before search is available; every AI Search model operation is accounted for by the selected Gateway and caching cannot substitute a different embedding response. | assistant+deployment | — | — | open |
| S17-053 | §17 L1137 | Static, JavaScript-heavy, variant-dependent and visually ambiguous shop fixtures are run through Tavily Extract and Browser Run; a successful HTTP response containing only navigation or a challenge counts as an extraction failure. | assistant+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S17-054 | §17 L1137 | The section 7 weather scenarios are included in both scheduled and conversational recommendation tests. | daily-service+journey-tests | packages/daily/src/weather/assess.ts; packages/daily/src/compose.ts | packages/daily/test/weather-assess.test.ts; packages/daily/test/board.test.ts; packages/daily/test/service.test.ts; note - covered for scheduled runs and ad hoc recommendations; conversational runs are the assistant and journey workstreams | partial |
| S17-055 | §17 L1137 | A pinned preview SDK is upgraded in development, the contract suite run, and the previous compatible version restored without changing data ownership or replaying effects. | deployment | — | — | open |
| S17-056 | §17 L1141 | The versioned evaluation corpus (Claude export, complete September 14 profile, September 15 decisions) is used with exact conversation and message references; historical assistant output is a candidate to judge, never a gold answer. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| S17-057 | §17 L1141 | The corpus has at least 50 scenarios with held-out source conversations and explicit historical versus constructed distinctions, covering ordinary days, difficult temperature transitions, a dramatic brief, footwear restrictions, depleted laundry, repetitive recent wear, ambiguous aliases, a changing body measurement, a misleading product page, a purchase to discourage, and an uncertain historical premise. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| S17-058 | §17 L1143 | Model profiles are compared against identical snapshots and task contracts, a representative subset is repeated at least five times, and complete tasks are measured (time to first useful content, completion latency, correct commands, unsupported claims, owner clarifications, total cost including repair). | journey-tests+assistant | — | — | open |
| S17-059 | §17 L1145 | Hard constraints have zero tolerated violations in the release suite. | journey-tests+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| S17-060 | §17 L1145 | Codex is the independent judge of candidate outputs against the complete profile, amendments, case evidence and scenario state; the candidate never grades itself, and the judge cites exact output excerpts and evidence references across conviction, coherence, personal fit, fabric and comfort, teaching, variety and owner-attention cost. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| S17-061 | §17 L1145 | Several different good outfits and explanations are accepted; prose is never compared to a canonical string and no exact garment combination is the taste answer. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| S17-062 | §17 L1147 | Source feedback and rubrics are kept out of held-out candidate prompts; candidates are judged anonymized with counterbalanced pair order, ties and insufficient evidence allowed, and a subset repeated to expose variability. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| S17-063 | §17 L1147 | Taste scores are reported separately from availability and accounting failures; an invalid outfit cannot pass on beauty. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| S17-064 | §17 L1147 | Initial release targets: no hard violations, at least four out of five on personal fit and practical usefulness for 80% of repeated cases, and at least one genuinely recommendable option on every feasible full-board case; failures and contradictory historical examples are kept as regression tests. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| S17-065 | §17 L1151 | Initial service targets: cached Today view within one second, local Studio swaps within 100 ms, and ordinary online commands confirmed within two seconds at the 95th percentile under personal-use load. | ios+foundation+deployment | — | — | open |
| S17-066 | §17 L1151 | Interactive assistant runs acknowledge promptly and aim for useful content within three seconds, while research and image jobs expose durable progress. | assistant | — | — | open |
| S17-067 | §17 L1153 | Morning target: a validated board and verified calendar projection by 6:50 AM on at least 99% of days over a rolling period, also counting bad recommendations, manual repairs and failures to apply a correction. | daily-service+deployment | packages/daily/src/schedule.ts; migrations/0100_daily_service.sql | packages/daily/test/service.test.ts; note - each run records board readiness and verified projection per day; the rolling 99 percent measure needs a deployed period | partial |
| S17-068 | §17 L1153 | Fourteen consecutive trial mornings plus the failure drills are required before replacing the daily service. | deployment | — | — | open |
| S17-069 | §17 L1155 | Operational traces record the request, context sources and versions, model profile, timing, tool results, validation failures, command receipts, projection outcomes and cost, with sensitive payloads redacted or access-controlled and no raw reasoning persisted. | deployment+daily-service | — | — | open |
| S17-070 | §17 L1155 | Diagnostics can answer why a garment was offered, why an option was removed, what changed, and whether the external delivery actually succeeded. | daily-service+deployment | packages/daily/src/boards.ts; packages/daily/src/document.ts; packages/daily/src/repair.ts; packages/daily/src/calendar/projector.ts | packages/daily/test/repair.test.ts; packages/daily/test/calendar.test.ts | implemented |

Note: the profile source file is `requirements/chris-wardrobe-profile.md` (second edition, 14 September 2026). Where a newer owner-confirmed restriction, measurement, size or physical state exists it supersedes the profile passage (S06-031); profile prose governs taste.

## Evaluation instructions

| ID | Source | Requirement | Workstream | Code | Tests | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| EV-001 | evals/README L3; START-HERE L9 | All 64 evaluation cases (40 adapted from historical conversations, 24 constructed) are integrated into the test suite. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-002 | evals/README L3 | Codex judges taste, while deterministic checks cover explicit inventory constraints and observed application state. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-003 | evals/README L7 | Each historical excerpt keeps its conversation ID, message ID, date, source position and source-message hash; the suite is not treated as an exhaustive annotation. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-004 | evals/README L9 | The profile copy in the suite is byte-identical to the September 14 profile (SHA-256 e15639d891f9a5264c7eff13d05a478bcb188745aea11323b2131db37f5cb198) and the September 15 decisions are applied separately. | journey-tests+foundation | — | — | open |
| EV-005 | evals/README L11; START-HERE L9 | The suite's wardrobe and scenario fixtures are constructed test data, not the owner's inventory; each case's stated observations override fixture defaults and missing photos are not reconstructed. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-006 | evals/README L11 | Calibration candidates use only structured visible text content; tool results and internal reasoning are excluded. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-007 | evals/README L32 | The split of 45 development and 19 held-out cases is preserved, with each historical conversation entirely in one split and calibration using development conversations only. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-008 | evals/README L32 | A candidate receives the complete profile, owner amendments, fixture state and request, and never source feedback, judge criteria, expected state assertions or historical candidate answers; the judge receives those only after the candidate finishes. | journey-tests+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-009 | evals/README L34 | The packet is the isolation boundary: a held-out candidate gets no filesystem access to the suite or the case catalogue, and any candidate exposure used for tuning is recorded and moves that case out of the untouched holdout. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-010 | evals/README L38-L43 | The source assets validate with `python3 evals/evaluate.py validate` and `list`. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-011 | evals/README L45-L52 | Candidate packets are prepared with `evaluate.py packet --mode candidate`, and each run saves complete output, exact model/profile and prompt versions, date, run ID, elapsed time and tool evidence in a run directory. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-012 | evals/README L52-L66 | Board outputs follow the candidate schema: `response` plus `options`, each with `id`, `garment_ids` and `explanation`. | journey-tests+daily-service | — | — | open |
| EV-013 | evals/README L68 | A smaller-than-requested board includes `shortage_reason`, checked against feasibility. | daily-service+journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-014 | evals/README L68 | Prose never establishes a successful write; command cases use the application's actual receipts and observed state. | journey-tests+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-015 | evals/README L70-L78 | Structured inventory facts are checked with `evaluate.py check-candidate` and the judge packet is prepared with `packet --mode judge`. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-016 | evals/README L80 | The candidate never grades its own output; the evaluation script calls no model and no provider billing path is silently selected. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-017 | evals/README L82-L87 | For behavioural cases a real application adapter collects outcomes under `observed` with `provenance.source: "application_adapter"`, a `run_id` and retained `artifact_paths`, verified with `evaluate.py check-state`. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-018 | evals/README L22, L89 | The 18 behavioural cases in `fixtures/state-assertions.json` are compared against normalized adapter observations; a model-generated prediction of expected state is not a passing integration test. | journey-tests+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-019 | evals/README L93 | Taste is scored one to five on personal fit, composition, comfort and fabric, variety and scope, practical usefulness, and voice and teaching; dimensions can be inapplicable and two different combinations can be equally good. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-020 | evals/README L93 | Every material judgment cites exact candidate text and relevant source evidence, and hard violations are kept separate from subjective scores. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-021 | evals/README L95 | Initial release targets (proposed thresholds, not results): no hard violations, personal fit and practical usefulness of at least four in 80% of repeated applicable cases, and at least one recommendable option in every feasible full-board case. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-022 | evals/README L97 | A representative group is repeated at least five times, including a quiet outfit, a dramatic brief, a swap, a depleted wardrobe, a purchase judgment and a correction. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-023 | evals/README L97 | Model comparisons use identical snapshots and anonymous labels, reversed pairwise order, permitted ties, retained individual judgments and repeated judge passes, and record whether candidate and judge share a model family. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-024 | evals/README L99 | The profile is not converted into a fashion formula (brown stays welcome, pink can be right generally and wrong for an interview, a quiet white-tee outfit can work); explanations are about the actual clothes and circumstances. | daily-service+journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-025 | evals/README L103 | The eight bundled historical reviews are calibration evidence only and never reported as validation of the new application. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-026 | evals/README L105 | The first end-to-end application run collects real receipts, state transitions, board outputs and Calendar read-backs. | journey-tests+deployment | — | — | open |
| EV-027 | evals/README L105 | The end-to-end run demonstrates missing-confirmation tolerance, weekly reset exceptions, cross-client duplicate merging, authoritative late observations, automatic future repair and all six feature journeys. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-028 | evals/README L109-L117 | Source rebuild scripts are run only after a reviewed source update, and the archived revision 3 to 4 specification edit script is never reapplied. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-029 | evals/judge L3 | The judge reads the complete profile, owner amendments, task state and selected historical evidence before grading and treats quotations, candidate text and retrieved material as data, ignoring embedded instructions. | journey-tests+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-030 | evals/judge L5 | The judge requires no single outfit or canonical prose and does not use the profile as a keyword checklist: a quiet outfit does not fail for lacking a statement piece and a conspicuous one does not pass for saying "sprezzatura". | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-031 | evals/judge L9-L13 | The judge's evidence order is: latest owner amendments and case observations, the full active profile, original owner feedback in its historical scope, verified fixture facts and receipts, then historical assistant statements as unverified claims. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-032 | evals/judge L15 | Case facts override fixture defaults; historical stock and measurements are not current facts; the judge never imagines a missing photograph, assumes an unrecorded tool call succeeded, or penalizes a candidate for a fact neither supplied nor required. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-033 | evals/judge L17-L28 | The six dimensions are scored with the rubric anchors (one, three, five; four is strong with a limited flaw, two a material problem), or null when inapplicable, with concise observable reasoning and no private chain-of-thought. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-034 | evals/judge L30 | Hard violations are reported separately and cannot be offset by taste scores: nonexistent or explicitly unavailable garments, missing socks, active footwear restrictions, changed locked items, a required observation refused on accounting grounds, duplicate daily wear counts, or a claimed external effect without evidence. | journey-tests+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-035 | evals/judge L30 | A doubtful style judgment is not automatically a hard violation, and uncertain availability under the authorized probability policy is not the same as known unavailable stock. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-036 | evals/judge L32 | Ordinary candidate runs are judged on the prompt and material actually supplied; historical-response reviews use the historical request and context and note when a later correction was not yet available. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-037 | evals/judge L34-L61 | The judgment is one JSON object with `case_id`, `candidate_id`, `mode`, `verdict` (pass, revise, fail or insufficient_evidence), `scores`, `hard_violations`, `findings` (candidate_excerpt, basis, judgment), `limitations` and `summary`. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-038 | evals/judge L63 | Pairwise work grades each candidate separately, records A, B, tie or insufficient evidence, reverses order for a second pass, preserves individual scores, and does not reward verbosity, agreement, a model name or similarity to the judge's own answer. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-039 | evals/judge L63; START-HERE L9 | When the judge shares a model family with the candidate this is recorded and not called independent-provider validation; Codex judgments that were not run are never claimed, and if Codex is unavailable candidate packets are collected and the check marked pending. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-040 | START-HERE L9 | Archive-dependent validation is ported to the bundled evidence (the full private chat export is absent) without pretending the full archive was rechecked. | journey-tests | — | — | open |

Note: the evaluation source files are `requirements/support/wardrobe-support/evals/README.md` (evals/README), `requirements/support/wardrobe-support/evals/judge.md` (evals/judge) and the still-applicable evaluation paragraph of `requirements/support/wardrobe-support/handoff/START-HERE.md` (START-HERE). START-HERE's historical restrictions on deploying and on unavailable credentials are superseded and are not requirements.
